2. In W.P.No.2980/2025, petitioners have sought for
issue of a writ of certiorari to quash the notification dated

College and Technical Education inviting applications for
selection to the post of Guest Lecturers for the academic year
2024-25. They have sought for a direction to the respondent
No.1 to provide relaxation of the UGC Regulations on Minimum
Qualifications for Appointment of Teachers and other Academic
Staff in Universities and Colleges and Measures for the
Maintenance of Standards in Higher Education, 2018 (for short,
‘the UGC Regulations, 2018’), in so far as it relates to
prescribing UGC-NET/SLET/SET exams as a pre-requisite for
appointment to the post of Guest Lecturers. They have also
sought for a direction to the respondent Nos.4 to 8 not to
discontinue their service in the respondent - Colleges.

15. The petitioners in these petitions claim that they
were recruited as Guest Lecturers pursuant to a notification
issued by the State Government. Petitioners in some petitions
claim that they are eligible and qualified to be appointed as
Assistant Professors in Degree Colleges. The petitioners claim
that the Commissioner, Department of College and Technical
Education, has been issuing notifications every year and
recruiting Guest Lecturers for a period of 10 months, at the end
of which they were relieved and reappointed during the next
academic year. They contend that during the academic year
2024-25, the notification dated 30.08.2024 was issued by the
Commissioner, pursuant to which they submitted application/s
and were reappointed for a period of 10 months. In the
meanwhile, this Court in W.P.No.23600/2023 had directed the
respondent Nos.1 and 2 therein to ensure full compliance of the
minimum qualification as prescribed by the University Grants
Commission under the UGC Regulations, 2018 before selecting

 - 389 -
 HC-KAR
NC: 2025:KHC:42266
WP No. 14074 of 2025
C/W WP No. 15293 of 2023
WP No. 16317 of 2023
AND 36 OTHERS
any candidate for the post of Guest Lecturers. This Court had
further directed the respondent Nos.1 and 2 therein not to
select any candidate who does not possess minimum
qualification as prescribed in the UGC Regulations, 2018 and
shall not select any candidate by placing a condition on him/her
that he/she shall obtain requisite qualification, namely,
NET/SLET/SET before 13.01.2025, unless he/she is awarded
with a Doctorate or is registered for a Doctoral program prior to
July 2009.
16. Petitioners in some petitions contend that they were
not made parties to the petition in W.P. No.23600/2023. They
all contend that they were appointed against sanctioned
permanent posts depending upon the student strength and the
workload for the Lecturers in the respective colleges. They
claim that the recruitment of Lecturers is not keeping pace with
the student strength in the respective colleges resulting in a
wide gap between the permanent teaching faculty and the
workload. They claim that in order to handle the workload,
locally available qualified candidates are employed on a year-to-year basis. They all claim that from the year 2013, selection

 - 390 -
 HC-KAR
NC: 2025:KHC:42266
WP No. 14074 of 2025
C/W WP No. 15293 of 2023
WP No. 16317 of 2023
AND 36 OTHERS
of guest faculty was based on merit and qualification. They
claim that after all the candidates who were qualified in
National Eligibility Test (NET) / Karnataka State Eligibility Test
(KSET) / State Eligibility Test (SET) and possessed Ph.D were
recruited, they were appointed as Guest Lecturers to fill in the
gap between the number of Lecturers required and the student
strength in the respective colleges. They claim that a High
Powered Committee was constituted to go into the question of
remedying the gap between the recruitment and workload and
the Committee recommended that the guest faculty be
assigned 19 hours of work for Science Teachers and 15 hours
for others. They all contend that they are more qualified than
fresh candidates and hence, their continuation in their
respective post(s) of Guest Lecturer(s) in respective College(s)
was imminent. They claim that the Commissioner, Department
of College and Technical Education, has issued a notification
dated 02.01.2025 following the order dated 05.09.2024 passed
by this Court in W.P No.23600/2023 as a result of which, the
petitioners are facing the threat of termination. The petitioners
are, therefore, before this Court, seeking the aforesaid reliefs.
Many of the petitioners have sought for regularisation of their

WP No. 16317 of 2023
AND 36 OTHERS
services, while some of them have sought for their continuation
in their respective post(s) in the respective College(s) till they
attain age of superannuation.
17. The learned senior counsel for the petitioners in
W.P. No. 3652/2025 submitted that the petitioners were not
heard in W.P. No.23600/2023 and therefore, the order dated
05.09.2024 passed by this Court cannot be held binding upon
the petitioners. In this regard he relied upon the judgment of
the Hon'ble Apex Court in Shivdeo Singh and others v. State
of Punjab and others [AIR 1963 SC 1909]. He contends
that the petitioners are all qualified personnel who have put in
long years of service in the respective college(s) and this Court
in W.P.No.3079/2022 and connected cases (disposed off on
11.01.2023) had recognized the same and held that their past
service to be counted at the rate of 3 marks for every year
subject to a maximum of 16 years. He contends that all the
petitioners are working at less than half the salary given to a
permanent Lecturer and therefore, the mandate of the UGC
Regulations, 2018 cannot be implemented in letter and spirit as
the same would be disastrous to the various Guest Lecturers

 - 392 -
 HC-KAR
NC: 2025:KHC:42266
WP No. 14074 of 2025
C/W WP No. 15293 of 2023
WP No. 16317 of 2023
AND 36 OTHERS
who are appointed. He contends that the respondent Nos.1 and
2 have not initiated the process for recruitment of Guest
Lecturers from a long time and therefore, the petitioners are all
at loss as they could neither apply for regular recruitment nor
could they secure NET/SET qualification. He, therefore,
contends that the earlier arrangement made by the State was
just and proper and prays that the same be continued.
18. The learned Additional Advocate General for the
respondent - State submitted that the State Government had
challenged the order passed by this Court dated 05.09.2024 in
W.P. No.23600/2023 before a Division Bench of this Court in
W.A. No.1578/2024. He contends that a Division Bench in
terms of the judgment dated 08.10.2025, has upheld the order
dated 05.09.2024 passed by this Court. He, therefore, submits
that the State Government has no other option than to fall in
line and comply with the order dated 05.09.2024 passed by this
Court in W.P. No.23600/2023. He thus submits, that all the
contentions raised by the petitioners in these petitions do not
survive for consideration.

 - 393 -
 HC-KAR
NC: 2025:KHC:42266
WP No. 14074 of 2025
C/W WP No. 15293 of 2023
WP No. 16317 of 2023
AND 36 OTHERS
19. This Court in W.P. No.23600/2023 after detailed
analysis of the provisions of law, more particularly, the UGC
Regulations, 2018 had in its order dated 05.09.2024, issued
the following directions:
"i) This writ petition is allowed in part.
ii) The respondent Nos.1 and 2 are directed to ensure
complete compliance of the minimum qualification
as prescribed by the UGC/respondent No.3 under
the UGC Regulations, 2018 before selecting any
candidates for the post of Guest Lecturer.
iii) It is made clear that in view of the order passed in
W.P.No.3079/2022 and connected cases, the marks
prescribed for past service for a maximum period of
16 years at the rate of 3 marks shall continue to be
applied.
iv) It is also made clear that the respondent Nos.1 and
2 shall not select any candidate who does not
possess minimum qualification as prescribed in the
UGC Regulations, 2018 and shall not select any
candidate by placing a condition on him/her that
he/she shall obtain requisite qualification namely
NET/SLET/SET before 13.01.2025, unless he/she is
awarded with a doctorate or is registered for a
doctoral program prior to July 2009."

 - 394 -
 HC-KAR
NC: 2025:KHC:42266
WP No. 14074 of 2025
C/W WP No. 15293 of 2023
WP No. 16317 of 2023
AND 36 OTHERS
20. The respondent Nos.1 and 2 in these petitions have
in the last many years ignored the UGC Regulations, 2018 with
impunity by recruiting Guest Lecturers in its Colleges
throughout the State of Karnataka on a yearly basis. These
Guest Lecturers did not possess the qualifications prescribed
under the UGC Regulations, 2018 but were continued from time
to time as it was convenient to the respondent Nos.1 and 2
since these Guest Lecturers were paid paltry sum of money as
salary, which was far less than what was paid to a regular
Lecturer in Colleges. One of the key functions of the State
Government is to provide education to its citizens and while
doing so, it should ensure that students in Colleges learn from
the best in the field. The purpose of the UGC Regulations, 2018
is to ensure that the Lecturer is sufficiently qualified and
empowered. The respondent Nos.1 and 2 have contrarily
recruited Guest Lecturers without following the reservation
roster and without applying the qualification prescribed under
the UGC Regulations, 2018. Now that the order dated
05.09.2024 passed by this Court in W.P. No.23600/2023 is
upheld by a Division Bench of this Court in W.A. No.1578/2024
(disposed off on 08.10.2025), all contentions urged by the

 - 395 -
 HC-KAR

WP No. 16317 of 2023
AND 36 OTHERS
petitioners in these petitions do not survive for consideration.
Therefore, these petitions fail and are dismissed.
21. In view of disposal of these petitions, the pending
interlocutory applications do not survive for consideration and
the same stand disposed off.
